`README.md`:

```md
# Android hardware attestation bypass — demo

This repository accompanies a [Quarkslab article](https://blog.quarkslab.com/bypassing-android-hardware-attestation.html) on Android hardware Key
Attestation and exists to illustrate it.

It is a small, end-to-end proof of concept that shows a simple bypass: an analyst on a rooted
phone defeats a backend's hardware attestation check by **relaying** the
attestation to a second, clean device. Nothing is forged. A genuine attestation
chain, produced by an unmodified device and bound to the backend's own nonce, is
forwarded in place of the rooted device's rejected chain.

> **Scope and ethics.** This is educational material for security research. It
> uses plain HTTP and disables safety checks on purpose ("demo only" markers
> throughout). Run it against your own devices and your own backend, on a
> trusted local network.

## How the bypass works

A rooted device's Keystore attestation reports an unlocked bootloader, so the
backend rejects it. Instead of attacking the crypto, the analyst intercepts the
app's own attestation call and swaps in a chain fetched live from a clean device
for the same challenge.

The hook replaces `KeystoreAttestation.generateAttestedKey(challenge)` in the
demo app and never calls the local Keystore, so the rooted device's chain is
never generated. The relayed chain is genuine and passes the backend's verified
boot and hardware-backed checks.

## Components

| Component | Path | Role | Runs on |
|-----------|------|------|---------|
| Demo client | [`apps/QuarkslabAttestationDemo/`](apps/QuarkslabAttestationDemo/README.md) | Target app. Requests a nonce, attests, sends the chain to the backend. Hooked by Frida. | Rooted analysis device |
| Attestation server | [`apps/QuarkslabAttestationServer/`](apps/QuarkslabAttestationServer/README.md) | Attestation oracle. Produces a genuine chain for a given nonce. Validates nothing. | Clean, unmodified device |
| Instrumentation | [`instrumentation/`](instrumentation/README.md) | Frida agent (the hook) plus the controller that relays the nonce to the clean device. | Analyst workstation + rooted device |
| Backend | [`backend/`](backend/README.md) | Relying party. Issues nonces and validates attestation chains. | Any machine reachable from the devices |

Each component has its own README with the full detail; this file ties them
together.

## Prerequisites

- **Two physical devices.** Attestation requires real hardware; emulators return
  a software-backed chain the backend rejects.
  - A **rooted** "analysis" device running `frida-server` 17.16.4, with the demo
    app installed.
  - A **clean, unmodified** device with the attestation server app installed
    (locked bootloader, StrongBox or TEE).
- A machine on the same network for the **backend** (Python 3, deps in
  `backend/requirements.txt`).
- A **JDK 17-21** to build the two Android apps, either from Android Studio or
  from the command line with the Gradle wrapper (`./gradlew assembleDebug`).
- **Node 20 or 22 LTS** on the workstation only if you rebuild the Frida agent.
  The compiled agent (`instrumentation/_agent.js`) is committed, so a rebuild is
  optional.
- All three (devices and backend) must reach each other over the network.

## End-to-end run

Bring the pieces up in this order.

1. **Backend.** On a machine reachable from both devices:
   ```bash
   cd backend
   pip install -r requirements.txt
   python3 attestation_backend.py      # listens on 0.0.0.0:8080
   ```
2. **Clean device.** Build and install the attestation server app, then open it,
   tap **START SERVER**, and note the URL it shows, e.g. `http://192.168.1.42:8080`.
3. **Rooted device.** Start `frida-server`, build and install the demo app, open
   it, and enter the backend address (`host:port`).

   Both apps build from Android Studio or from the command line with the Gradle
   wrapper (`./gradlew assembleDebug`, JDK 17-21); see each app's README for the
   details.
4. **Relay.** On the workstation:
   ```bash
   cd instrumentation
   python3 control.py --host <clean-device-ip> --port 8080
   ```
   The controller spawns the demo app, installs the hook, then resumes it.
5. **Trigger.** Tap **RUN ATTESTATION** in the demo app. The backend returns
   **valid**: the relayed chain reports a locked bootloader.
6. **Negative control.** Stop `control.py` and tap **RUN ATTESTATION** again.
   The demo app now sends the rooted device's own chain and the backend rejects
   it. This is the check the relay defeats.

Rebuilding the agent (only if you change `agent/agent.ts`):

```bash
cd instrumentation
nvm use          # Node 22 from .nvmrc
npm install
npm run build    # regenerates _agent.js
```

## Repository layout

```
apps/
  QuarkslabAttestationDemo/     target client app (Android Studio project)
  QuarkslabAttestationServer/   clean-device attestation oracle (Android Studio project)
instrumentation/                Frida agent + relay controller
backend/                        Python attestation validator
LICENSE                         MIT
```

`apps/QuarkslabAttestationDemo/README.md`:

```md
# Quarkslab Attestation Demo

> Part of the [Android hardware attestation bypass demo](../../README.md). See the root README for the full setup.

Android client for the Python Key Attestation backend. The app asks the backend for a
challenge, generates an attested key pair in the Android Keystore, and sends the resulting
certificate chain back for validation.

## Flow

| Step | Call | Payload |
|------|------|---------|
| 1 | `POST /nonce` | -> `{ "nonce": "<base64url>", "expires_in": 300 }` |
| 2 | Android Keystore | EC P-256 key, `setAttestationChallenge(base64url_decode(nonce))` |
| 3 | `POST /verify` | `{ "nonce": "<base64url>", "chain": ["<der_base64>", ...] }`, leaf first |
| 4 | verdict | `{ "valid": true, "hardware_backed": bool, "attestation": { ... } }` or `{ "valid": false, "error": "..." }` |

The nonce is echoed back verbatim in step 3 so the backend can match it in its store. The
challenge embedded in the certificate is the raw decoded value, which is what the backend
compares after re-encoding it as unpadded base64url.

## Build

Open the project in Android Studio Panda and sync. Application id `com.quarkslab.attestation.demo`.
AGP 8.13.0, Gradle 8.14.3, Kotlin 2.2.0, `compileSdk 36`, `minSdk 33`. No third party dependency:
all ASN.1 parsing happens server side.

Command line build (Gradle wrapper, no Android Studio needed):

```bash
./gradlew assembleDebug   # -> app/build/outputs/apk/debug/app-debug.apk
```

Requires a JDK 17-21 exported as `JAVA_HOME` (Android Studio's bundled JBR works; a newer JDK such
as 26 is not supported by Gradle 8.14). The SDK path is read from `local.properties` (`sdk.dir`) or
the `ANDROID_HOME` environment variable.

## Run

1. Start the Python backend on a machine reachable from the device: `python3 attestation_backend.py` (see [`backend/`](../../backend/README.md))
2. Launch the app, enter `host:port` (a full `http://host:port` URL also works), tap
   **RUN ATTESTATION**.

Plain HTTP is allowed through `res/xml/network_security_config.xml`. This is a demo only.

```

`apps/QuarkslabAttestationDemo/app/src/main/java/com/quarkslab/attestation/demo/BackendClient.kt`:

```kt
package com.quarkslab.attestation.demo

import org.json.JSONArray
import org.json.JSONObject
import java.net.HttpURLConnection
import java.net.URL

/** Raw HTTP response: status code plus the decoded JSON body. */
data class HttpResult(val status: Int, val body: JSONObject) {
    val isSuccess: Boolean get() = status in 200..299
}

/**
 * Minimal client for the Python attestation backend.
 *
 *   POST /nonce   -> { "nonce": "<base64url>", "expires_in": <seconds> }
 *   POST /verify  -> { "valid": true, "hardware_backed": <bool>, "attestation": { ... } }
 *                    { "valid": false, "error": "<reason>" }
 */
class BackendClient(server: String) {

    private val baseUrl: String = server.trim().trimEnd('/').let {
        if (it.startsWith("http://") || it.startsWith("https://")) it else "http://$it"
    }

    fun requestNonce(): String {
        val result = post("/nonce", JSONObject())
        if (!result.isSuccess) throw IllegalStateException("POST /nonce returned HTTP ${result.status}")
        return result.body.getString("nonce")
    }

    fun verify(nonce: String, chainBase64: List<String>): HttpResult =
        post("/verify", JSONObject().put("nonce", nonce).put("chain", JSONArray(chainBase64)))

    private fun post(path: String, payload: JSONObject): HttpResult {
        val connection = (URL(baseUrl + path).openConnection() as HttpURLConnection).apply {
            requestMethod = "POST"
            doOutput = true
            connectTimeout = 10_000
            readTimeout = 30_000
            setRequestProperty("Content-Type", "application/json")
        }
        try {
            connection.outputStream.use { it.write(payload.toString().toByteArray()) }
            val status = connection.responseCode
            val stream = if (status in 200..299) connection.inputStream else connection.errorStream
            val text = stream?.bufferedReader()?.use { it.readText() }.orEmpty()
            return HttpResult(status, JSONObject(text.ifBlank { "{}" }))
        } finally {
            connection.disconnect()
        }
    }
}

```

`apps/QuarkslabAttestationDemo/app/src/main/java/com/quarkslab/attestation/demo/KeystoreAttestation.kt`:

```kt
package com.quarkslab.attestation.demo

import android.content.Context
import android.content.pm.PackageManager
import android.security.keystore.KeyGenParameterSpec
import android.security.keystore.KeyProperties
import android.util.Base64
import java.security.KeyPairGenerator
import java.security.KeyStore
import java.security.cert.X509Certificate
import java.security.spec.ECGenParameterSpec

private const val KEY_ALIAS = "qb_attestation_demo_key"

/** Outcome of the local key generation step. */
data class AttestedKey(
    /** DER certificates, standard base64, leaf first, as expected by POST /verify. */
    val chainBase64: List<String>,
    /** Security level actually requested from the Keystore: StrongBox or TEE. */
    val requestedLevel: String,
    /** Set when StrongBox was advertised by the device but refused the key. */
    val fallbackReason: String?
)

/**
 * Generates an EC P-256 key pair inside the Android Keystore with the backend nonce
 * as attestation challenge, then exports the resulting certificate chain.
 */
class KeystoreAttestation(private val context: Context) {

    fun generateAttestedKey(challenge: ByteArray): AttestedKey {
        deleteKey()

        var requestedLevel = "TEE"
        var fallbackReason: String? = null

        val strongBoxAdvertised = context.packageManager
            .hasSystemFeature(PackageManager.FEATURE_STRONGBOX_KEYSTORE)

        if (strongBoxAdvertised) {
            try {
                generateKey(challenge, strongBox = true)
                requestedLevel = "StrongBox"
            } catch (e: Exception) {
                // Several devices advertise StrongBox but reject attested keys on it.
                fallbackReason = "StrongBox refused the key: ${e.message}"
                generateKey(challenge, strongBox = false)
            }
        } else {
            generateKey(challenge, strongBox = false)
        }

        val keyStore = KeyStore.getInstance("AndroidKeyStore").apply { load(null) }
        val chain = keyStore.getCertificateChain(KEY_ALIAS)
            ?: throw IllegalStateException("Keystore returned no certificate chain")

        return AttestedKey(
            chainBase64 = chain.map {
                Base64.encodeToString((it as X509Certificate).encoded, Base64.NO_WRAP)
            },
            requestedLevel = requestedLevel,
            fallbackReason = fallbackReason
        )
    }

    private fun generateKey(challenge: ByteArray, strongBox: Boolean) {
        val spec = KeyGenParameterSpec.Builder(
            KEY_ALIAS,
            KeyProperties.PURPOSE_SIGN or KeyProperties.PURPOSE_VERIFY
        )
            .setAlgorithmParameterSpec(ECGenParameterSpec("secp256r1"))
            .setDigests(KeyProperties.DIGEST_SHA256)
            .setAttestationChallenge(challenge)
            .setIsStrongBoxBacked(strongBox)
            .build()

        KeyPairGenerator.getInstance(KeyProperties.KEY_ALGORITHM_EC, "AndroidKeyStore").apply {
            initialize(spec)
            generateKeyPair()
        }
    }

    private fun deleteKey() {
        runCatching {
            KeyStore.getInstance("AndroidKeyStore").apply { load(null) }.deleteEntry(KEY_ALIAS)
        }
    }
}

```

`apps/QuarkslabAttestationDemo/app/src/main/java/com/quarkslab/attestation/demo/MainActivity.kt`:

```kt
package com.quarkslab.attestation.demo

import android.content.Context
import android.os.Bundle
import android.util.Base64
import android.view.View
import androidx.appcompat.app.AppCompatActivity
import androidx.lifecycle.lifecycleScope
import com.quarkslab.attestation.demo.databinding.ActivityMainBinding
import kotlinx.coroutines.Dispatchers
import kotlinx.coroutines.launch
import kotlinx.coroutines.withContext
import org.json.JSONObject

private const val PREFS = "qb_attestation"
private const val PREF_SERVER = "server"

/** Result of a full attestation round trip, ready to be rendered. */
private data class Report(val trusted: Boolean, val headline: String, val details: String)

class MainActivity : AppCompatActivity() {

    private lateinit var binding: ActivityMainBinding

    override fun onCreate(savedInstanceState: Bundle?) {
        super.onCreate(savedInstanceState)
        binding = ActivityMainBinding.inflate(layoutInflater)
        setContentView(binding.root)

        val prefs = getSharedPreferences(PREFS, Context.MODE_PRIVATE)
        binding.etServer.setText(prefs.getString(PREF_SERVER, getString(R.string.server_default)))

        binding.btnAttest.setOnClickListener {
            val server = binding.etServer.text.toString().trim()
            if (server.isEmpty()) return@setOnClickListener
            prefs.edit().putString(PREF_SERVER, server).apply()
            runAttestation(server)
        }
    }

    private fun runAttestation(server: String) {
        binding.btnAttest.isEnabled = false
        binding.progress.visibility = View.VISIBLE
        binding.cardResult.visibility = View.GONE

        lifecycleScope.launch {
            val report = withContext(Dispatchers.IO) { attest(server) }
            render(report)
            binding.progress.visibility = View.GONE
            binding.btnAttest.isEnabled = true
        }
    }

    /**
     * 1. POST /nonce   -> challenge issued by the backend
     * 2. Keystore      -> attested key pair bound to that challenge
     * 3. POST /verify  -> chain validation and verdict
     */
    private fun attest(server: String): Report = try {
        val backend = BackendClient(server)

        val nonce = backend.requestNonce()
        // The backend encodes the nonce as unpadded base64url; the challenge is the raw value.
        val challenge = Base64.decode(nonce, Base64.URL_SAFE or Base64.NO_WRAP)

        val key = KeystoreAttestation(this).generateAttestedKey(challenge)
        val response = backend.verify(nonce, key.chainBase64)

        buildReport(nonce, key, response)
    } catch (e: Exception) {
        Report(false, "LOCAL FAILURE", "${e.javaClass.simpleName}\n${e.message ?: "no message"}")
    }

    private fun buildReport(nonce: String, key: AttestedKey, response: HttpResult): Report {
        val valid = response.isSuccess && response.body.optBoolean("valid", false)

        val details = buildString {
            appendLine("[ BACKEND RESPONSE ]  HTTP ${response.status}")
            appendLine("  valid            $valid")
            if (response.body.has("hardware_backed")) {
                appendLine("  hardware_backed  ${response.body.optBoolean("hardware_backed")}")
            }
            response.body.optString("error").takeIf { it.isNotEmpty() }?.let {
                appendLine("  error            $it")
            }
            appendLine()

            response.body.optJSONObject("attestation")?.let { attestation ->
                appendLine("[ ATTESTATION ]")
                appendLine(formatJson(attestation))
                appendLine()
            }

            appendLine("[ LOCAL KEY GENERATION ]")
            appendLine("  requested_level  ${key.requestedLevel}")
            key.fallbackReason?.let { appendLine("  fallback         $it") }
            appendLine("  chain_length     ${key.chainBase64.size} certificate(s)")
            appendLine("  nonce            ${nonce.take(24)}...")
        }.trim()

        val headline = when {
            valid -> "ATTESTATION VALID"
            response.status in 200..299 -> "ATTESTATION REJECTED"
            else -> "ATTESTATION REJECTED  (HTTP ${response.status})"
        }
        return Report(valid, headline, details)
    }

    /** Flattens a one level JSON object into aligned key/value lines. */
    private fun formatJson(json: JSONObject): String {
        val keys = json.keys().asSequence().toList()
        val width = keys.maxOfOrNull { it.length } ?: 0
        return keys.joinToString("\n") { "  ${it.padEnd(width)}  ${json.opt(it)}" }
    }

    private fun render(report: Report) {
        binding.cardResult.visibility = View.VISIBLE
        binding.cardResult.setCardBackgroundColor(
            getColor(if (report.trusted) R.color.qb_valid_bg else R.color.qb_invalid_bg)
        )
        binding.tvVerdict.text = report.headline
        binding.tvVerdict.setTextColor(
            getColor(if (report.trusted) R.color.qb_blue else R.color.qb_orange)
        )
        binding.tvDetails.text = report.details
    }
}

`apps/QuarkslabAttestationDemo/gradlew`:

```
#!/bin/sh

#
# Copyright © 2015-2021 the original authors.
#
# Licensed under the Apache License, Version 2.0 (the "License");
# you may not use this file except in compliance with the License.
# You may obtain a copy of the License at
#
#      https://www.apache.org/licenses/LICENSE-2.0
#
# Unless required by applicable law or agreed to in writing, software
# distributed under the License is distributed on an "AS IS" BASIS,
# WITHOUT WARRANTIES OR CONDITIONS OF ANY KIND, either express or implied.
# See the License for the specific language governing permissions and
# limitations under the License.
#
# SPDX-License-Identifier: Apache-2.0
#

##############################################################################
#
#   Gradle start up script for POSIX generated by Gradle.
#
#   Important for running:
#
#   (1) You need a POSIX-compliant shell to run this script. If your /bin/sh is
#       noncompliant, but you have some other compliant shell such as ksh or
#       bash, then to run this script, type that shell name before the whole
#       command line, like:
#
#           ksh Gradle
#
#       Busybox and similar reduced shells will NOT work, because this script
#       requires all of these POSIX shell features:
#         * functions;
#         * expansions «$var», «${var}», «${var:-default}», «${var+SET}»,
#           «${var#prefix}», «${var%suffix}», and «$( cmd )»;
#         * compound commands having a testable exit status, especially «case»;
#         * various built-in commands including «command», «set», and «ulimit».
#
#   Important for patching:
#
#   (2) This script targets any POSIX shell, so it avoids extensions provided
#       by Bash, Ksh, etc; in particular arrays are avoided.
#
#       The "traditional" practice of packing multiple parameters into a
#       space-separated string is a well documented source of bugs and security
#       problems, so this is (mostly) avoided, by progressively accumulating
#       options in "$@", and eventually passing that to Java.
#
#       Where the inherited environment variables (DEFAULT_JVM_OPTS, JAVA_OPTS,
#       and GRADLE_OPTS) rely on word-splitting, this is performed explicitly;
#       see the in-line comments for details.
#
#       There are tweaks for specific operating systems such as AIX, CygWin,
#       Darwin, MinGW, and NonStop.
#
#   (3) This script is generated from the Groovy template
#       https://github.com/gradle/gradle/blob/HEAD/platforms/jvm/plugins-application/src/main/resources/org/gradle/api/internal/plugins/unixStartScript.txt
#       within the Gradle project.
#
#       You can find Gradle at https://github.com/gradle/gradle/.
#
##############################################################################

# Attempt to set APP_HOME

# Resolve links: $0 may be a link
app_path=$0

# Need this for daisy-chained symlinks.
while
    APP_HOME=${app_path%"${app_path##*/}"}  # leaves a trailing /; empty if no leading path
    [ -h "$app_path" ]
do
    ls=$( ls -ld "$app_path" )
    link=${ls#*' -> '}
    case $link in             #(
      /*)   app_path=$link ;; #(
      *)    app_path=$APP_HOME$link ;;
    esac
done

# This is normally unused
# shellcheck disable=SC2034
APP_BASE_NAME=${0##*/}
# Discard cd standard output in case $CDPATH is set (https://github.com/gradle/gradle/issues/25036)
APP_HOME=$( cd -P "${APP_HOME:-./}" > /dev/null && printf '%s\n' "$PWD" ) || exit

# Use the maximum available, or set MAX_FD != -1 to use that value.
MAX_FD=maximum

warn () {
    echo "$*"
} >&2

die () {
    echo
    echo "$*"
    echo
    exit 1
} >&2

# OS specific support (must be 'true' or 'false').
cygwin=false
msys=false
darwin=false
nonstop=false
case "$( uname )" in                #(
  CYGWIN* )         cygwin=true  ;; #(
  Darwin* )         darwin=true  ;; #(
  MSYS* | MINGW* )  msys=true    ;; #(
  NONSTOP* )        nonstop=true ;;
esac

CLASSPATH="\\\"\\\""


# Determine the Java command to use to start the JVM.
if [ -n "$JAVA_HOME" ] ; then
    if [ -x "$JAVA_HOME/jre/sh/java" ] ; then
        # IBM's JDK on AIX uses strange locations for the executables
        JAVACMD=$JAVA_HOME/jre/sh/java
    else
        JAVACMD=$JAVA_HOME/bin/java
    fi
    if [ ! -x "$JAVACMD" ] ; then
        die "ERROR: JAVA_HOME is set to an invalid directory: $JAVA_HOME

Please set the JAVA_HOME variable in your environment to match the
location of your Java installation."
    fi
else
    JAVACMD=java
    if ! command -v java >/dev/null 2>&1
    then
        die "ERROR: JAVA_HOME is not set and no 'java' command could be found in your PATH.

Please set the JAVA_HOME variable in your environment to match the
location of your Java installation."
    fi
fi

# Increase the maximum file descriptors if we can.
if ! "$cygwin" && ! "$darwin" && ! "$nonstop" ; then
    case $MAX_FD in #(
      max*)
        # In POSIX sh, ulimit -H is undefined. That's why the result is checked to see if it worked.
        # shellcheck disable=SC2039,SC3045
        MAX_FD=$( ulimit -H -n ) ||
            warn "Could not query maximum file descriptor limit"
    esac
    case $MAX_FD in  #(
      '' | soft) :;; #(
      *)
        # In POSIX sh, ulimit -n is undefined. That's why the result is checked to see if it worked.
        # shellcheck disable=SC2039,SC3045
        ulimit -n "$MAX_FD" ||
            warn "Could not set maximum file descriptor limit to $MAX_FD"
    esac
fi

# Collect all arguments for the java command, stacking in reverse order:
#   * args from the command line
#   * the main class name
#   * -classpath
#   * -D...appname settings
#   * --module-path (only if needed)
#   * DEFAULT_JVM_OPTS, JAVA_OPTS, and GRADLE_OPTS environment variables.

# For Cygwin or MSYS, switch paths to Windows format before running java
if "$cygwin" || "$msys" ; then
    APP_HOME=$( cygpath --path --mixed "$APP_HOME" )
    CLASSPATH=$( cygpath --path --mixed "$CLASSPATH" )

    JAVACMD=$( cygpath --unix "$JAVACMD" )

    # Now convert the arguments - kludge to limit ourselves to /bin/sh
    for arg do
        if
            case $arg in                                #(
              -*)   false ;;                            # don't mess with options #(
              /?*)  t=${arg#/} t=/${t%%/*}              # looks like a POSIX filepath
                    [ -e "$t" ] ;;                      #(
              *)    false ;;
            esac
        then
            arg=$( cygpath --path --ignore --mixed "$arg" )
        fi
        # Roll the args list around exactly as many times as the number of
        # args, so each arg winds up back in the position where it started, but
        # possibly modified.
        #
        # NB: a `for` loop captures its iteration list before it begins, so
        # changing the positional parameters here affects neither the number of
        # iterations, nor the values presented in `arg`.
        shift                   # remove old arg
        set -- "$@" "$arg"      # push replacement arg
    done
fi


# Add default JVM options here. You can also use JAVA_OPTS and GRADLE_OPTS to pass JVM options to this script.
DEFAULT_JVM_OPTS='"-Xmx64m" "-Xms64m"'

# Collect all arguments for the java command:
#   * DEFAULT_JVM_OPTS, JAVA_OPTS, and optsEnvironmentVar are not allowed to contain shell fragments,
#     and any embedded shellness will be escaped.
#   * For example: A user cannot expect ${Hostname} to be expanded, as it is an environment variable and will be
#     treated as '${Hostname}' itself on the command line.

set -- \
        "-Dorg.gradle.appname=$APP_BASE_NAME" \
        -classpath "$CLASSPATH" \
        -jar "$APP_HOME/gradle/wrapper/gradle-wrapper.jar" \
        "$@"

# Stop when "xargs" is not available.
if ! command -v xargs >/dev/null 2>&1
then
    die "xargs is not available"
fi

# Use "xargs" to parse quoted args.
#
# With -n1 it outputs one arg per line, with the quotes and backslashes removed.
#
# In Bash we could simply go:
#
#   readarray ARGS < <( xargs -n1 <<<"$var" ) &&
#   set -- "${ARGS[@]}" "$@"
#
# but POSIX shell has neither arrays nor command substitution, so instead we
# post-process each arg (as a line of input to sed) to backslash-escape any
# character that might be a shell metacharacter, then use eval to reverse
# that process (while maintaining the separation between arguments), and wrap
# the whole thing up as a single "set" statement.
#
# This will of course break if any of these variables contains a newline or
# an unmatched quote.
#

eval "set -- $(
        printf '%s\n' "$DEFAULT_JVM_OPTS $JAVA_OPTS $GRADLE_OPTS" |
        xargs -n1 |
        sed ' s~[^-[:alnum:]+,./:=@_]~\\&~g; ' |
        tr '\n' ' '
    )" '"$@"'

exec "$JAVACMD" "$@"

```

`apps/QuarkslabAttestationDemo/gradlew.bat`:

```bat
@rem
@rem Copyright 2015 the original author or authors.
@rem
@rem Licensed under the Apache License, Version 2.0 (the "License");
@rem you may not use this file except in compliance with the License.
@rem You may obtain a copy of the License at
@rem
@rem      https://www.apache.org/licenses/LICENSE-2.0
@rem
@rem Unless required by applicable law or agreed to in writing, software
@rem distributed under the License is distributed on an "AS IS" BASIS,
@rem WITHOUT WARRANTIES OR CONDITIONS OF ANY KIND, either express or implied.
@rem See the License for the specific language governing permissions and
@rem limitations under the License.
@rem
@rem SPDX-License-Identifier: Apache-2.0
@rem

@if "%DEBUG%"=="" @echo off
@rem ##########################################################################
@rem
@rem  Gradle startup script for Windows
@rem
@rem ##########################################################################

@rem Set local scope for the variables with windows NT shell
if "%OS%"=="Windows_NT" setlocal

set DIRNAME=%~dp0
if "%DIRNAME%"=="" set DIRNAME=.
@rem This is normally unused
set APP_BASE_NAME=%~n0
set APP_HOME=%DIRNAME%

@rem Resolve any "." and ".." in APP_HOME to make it shorter.
for %%i in ("%APP_HOME%") do set APP_HOME=%%~fi

@rem Add default JVM options here. You can also use JAVA_OPTS and GRADLE_OPTS to pass JVM options to this script.
set DEFAULT_JVM_OPTS="-Xmx64m" "-Xms64m"

@rem Find java.exe
if defined JAVA_HOME goto findJavaFromJavaHome

set JAVA_EXE=java.exe
%JAVA_EXE% -version >NUL 2>&1
if %ERRORLEVEL% equ 0 goto execute

echo. 1>&2
echo ERROR: JAVA_HOME is not set and no 'java' command could be found in your PATH. 1>&2
echo. 1>&2
echo Please set the JAVA_HOME variable in your environment to match the 1>&2
echo location of your Java installation. 1>&2

goto fail

:findJavaFromJavaHome
set JAVA_HOME=%JAVA_HOME:"=%
set JAVA_EXE=%JAVA_HOME%/bin/java.exe

if exist "%JAVA_EXE%" goto execute

echo. 1>&2
echo ERROR: JAVA_HOME is set to an invalid directory: %JAVA_HOME% 1>&2
echo. 1>&2
echo Please set the JAVA_HOME variable in your environment to match the 1>&2
echo location of your Java installation. 1>&2

goto fail

:execute
@rem Setup the command line

set CLASSPATH=


@rem Execute Gradle
"%JAVA_EXE%" %DEFAULT_JVM_OPTS% %JAVA_OPTS% %GRADLE_OPTS% "-Dorg.gradle.appname=%APP_BASE_NAME%" -classpath "%CLASSPATH%" -jar "%APP_HOME%\gradle\wrapper\gradle-wrapper.jar" %*

:end
@rem End local scope for the variables with windows NT shell
if %ERRORLEVEL% equ 0 goto mainEnd

:fail
rem Set variable GRADLE_EXIT_CONSOLE if you need the _script_ return code instead of
rem the _cmd.exe /c_ return code!
set EXIT_CODE=%ERRORLEVEL%
if %EXIT_CODE% equ 0 set EXIT_CODE=1
if not ""=="%GRADLE_EXIT_CONSOLE%" exit %EXIT_CODE%
exit /b %EXIT_CODE%

:mainEnd
if "%OS%"=="Windows_NT" endlocal

:omega

```

`apps/QuarkslabAttestationServer/README.md`:

```md
# Quarkslab Attestation Server

> Part of the [Android hardware attestation bypass demo](../../README.md). See the root README for the full setup.

Local HTTP oracle for the Android Key Attestation demo. It runs on a clean,
unmodified device. Given a nonce it generates an attested key pair in the
Android Keystore and returns the certificate chain. It validates nothing, it
only produces chains.

Its response body is exactly the request body expected by the backend
`POST /verify` endpoint, so a relay can forward it as is.

## API

| Method | Path | Body / query | Response |
|--------|------|--------------|----------|
| GET  | `/status` | - | `{ "status": "ok", "port": 8080 }` |
| POST | `/attest` | `{ "nonce": "<base64url>" }` | `{ "nonce", "chain", "chain_length" }` |
| GET  | `/attest` | `?nonce=<base64url>` | same, for quick tests |

`chain` is a list of DER certificates, standard base64, leaf first, root last.

## Alignment with the backend

The nonce is the unpadded base64url string issued by the backend `POST /nonce`.
The server decodes it to raw bytes, passes it to `setAttestationChallenge`, and
echoes the same string back in `nonce`. The output object is a ready made
`/verify` request:

```
backend POST /nonce      -> { "nonce": "<base64url>", ... }
server  POST /attest      -> { "nonce": "<base64url>", "chain": [...] }
backend POST /verify      <- { "nonce": "<base64url>", "chain": [...] }
```

## Idle behaviour

The old build hung on requests once the phone went idle. The activity now sets
`FLAG_KEEP_SCREEN_ON`, the same flag video players use, so the device does not
enter idle and throttle the socket while the screen is open. The server is tied
to the activity lifecycle. Keep the app in the foreground while serving.

## Build

Open the project in Android Studio Panda and sync. Application id
`com.quarkslab.attestation.server`. AGP 8.13.0, Gradle 8.14.3, Kotlin 2.2.0,
`compileSdk 36`, `minSdk 33`. One dependency: NanoHTTPD 2.3.1.

Command line build (Gradle wrapper, no Android Studio needed):

```bash
./gradlew assembleDebug   # -> app/build/outputs/apk/debug/app-debug.apk
```

Requires a JDK 17-21 exported as `JAVA_HOME` (Android Studio's bundled JBR works; a newer JDK
such as 26 is not supported by Gradle 8.14). The SDK path is read from `local.properties`
(`sdk.dir`) or the `ANDROID_HOME` environment variable.

## Run

1. Launch the app on a clean device, set a port, tap START SERVER.
2. The card shows the reachable URL, for example `http://192.168.1.42:8080`.

Plain HTTP on the local network. Demo only.

## Test

```bash
curl http://192.168.1.42:8080/status

# nonce is the value returned by the backend POST /nonce
curl -X POST http://192.168.1.42:8080/attest \
  -H "Content-Type: application/json" \
  -d '{"nonce":"<base64url>"}'
```

## Source layout

Package `com.quarkslab.attestation.server`.

```
MainActivity.kt           UI, screen-on flag, start/stop, URL display
AttestationHttpServer.kt  NanoHTTPD routes and Keystore attestation
```

```

`apps/QuarkslabAttestationServer/app/build.gradle`:

```gradle
plugins {
    id 'com.android.application'
    id 'org.jetbrains.kotlin.android'
}

android {
    namespace 'com.quarkslab.attestation.server'
    compileSdk 36

    defaultConfig {
        applicationId "com.quarkslab.attestation.server"
        minSdk 33
        targetSdk 36
        versionCode 1
        versionName "1.0"
    }

    buildFeatures {
        viewBinding true
    }

    compileOptions {
        sourceCompatibility JavaVersion.VERSION_17
        targetCompatibility JavaVersion.VERSION_17
    }

    kotlinOptions {
        jvmTarget = '17'
    }
}

dependencies {
    implementation 'androidx.core:core-ktx:1.16.0'
    implementation 'androidx.appcompat:appcompat:1.7.1'
    implementation 'androidx.cardview:cardview:1.0.0'

    // Lightweight embedded HTTP server
    implementation 'org.nanohttpd:nanohttpd:2.3.1'

`apps/QuarkslabAttestationServer/app/src/main/java/com/quarkslab/attestation/server/AttestationHttpServer.kt`:

```kt
package com.quarkslab.attestation.server

import android.os.Build
import android.security.keystore.KeyGenParameterSpec
import android.security.keystore.KeyProperties
import android.util.Base64
import android.util.Log
import fi.iki.elonen.NanoHTTPD
import org.json.JSONArray
import org.json.JSONObject
import java.security.KeyPairGenerator
import java.security.KeyStore
import java.security.cert.X509Certificate
import java.security.spec.ECGenParameterSpec

/**
 * Local HTTP oracle. Given a nonce, it generates an attested key pair in the
 * Android Keystore and returns the certificate chain.
 *
 * The response body is exactly the request body expected by the backend
 * POST /verify endpoint, so a relay can forward it as is:
 *
 *   GET  /status          -> { "status": "ok", "port": <int> }
 *   POST /attest          -> body { "nonce": "<base64url>" }
 *   GET  /attest?nonce=..  -> same, for quick manual tests
 *
 *   200 -> { "nonce": "<base64url>", "chain": ["<der_base64>", ...], "chain_length": <int> }
 *   4xx -> { "error": "<code>", "message": "<text>" }
 *
 * The nonce format matches the backend: unpadded base64url, decoded to raw
 * bytes and passed as the attestation challenge, and echoed back verbatim so
 * the backend can match it in its store.
 */
class AttestationHttpServer(port: Int) : NanoHTTPD(port) {

    companion object {
        const val DEFAULT_PORT = 8080
        private const val TAG = "QbAttestationServer"
        private const val KEYSTORE = "AndroidKeyStore"
        private const val ALIAS_PREFIX = "qb_attest_"
        private const val MIME_JSON = "application/json"
    }

    override fun serve(session: IHTTPSession): Response {
        Log.d(TAG, "${session.method} ${session.uri}")
        return try {
            when {
                session.uri == "/status" && session.method == Method.GET ->
                    okJson(JSONObject().put("status", "ok").put("port", listeningPort))

                session.uri == "/attest" && session.method == Method.POST ->
                    handleAttestPost(session)

                session.uri == "/attest" && session.method == Method.GET ->
                    performAttestation(session.parameters["nonce"]?.firstOrNull())

                else -> error(Response.Status.NOT_FOUND, "NOT_FOUND", "Unknown endpoint ${session.uri}")
            }
        } catch (e: Exception) {
            Log.e(TAG, "Unhandled error", e)
            error(Response.Status.INTERNAL_ERROR, "INTERNAL_ERROR", e.message ?: "Unexpected error")
        }
    }

    private fun handleAttestPost(session: IHTTPSession): Response {
        val body = mutableMapOf<String, String>()
        session.parseBody(body)
        val raw = body["postData"].orEmpty()
        if (raw.isBlank()) return error(Response.Status.BAD_REQUEST, "MISSING_BODY", "Body must be JSON with a 'nonce' field")
        val nonce = try {
            JSONObject(raw).getString("nonce")
        } catch (e: Exception) {
            return error(Response.Status.BAD_REQUEST, "INVALID_JSON", "Could not parse JSON body: ${e.message}")
        }
        return performAttestation(nonce)
    }

    /**
     * Generates an ephemeral EC P-256 key with [nonce] as attestation challenge
     * and returns its certificate chain. The key is deleted afterwards.
     */
    private fun performAttestation(nonce: String?): Response {
        if (nonce.isNullOrBlank()) return error(Response.Status.BAD_REQUEST, "MISSING_NONCE", "Nonce is required")

        val challenge = decodeNonce(nonce)
            ?: return error(Response.Status.BAD_REQUEST, "INVALID_NONCE", "Nonce must be base64url")
        if (challenge.size < 8) return error(Response.Status.BAD_REQUEST, "NONCE_TOO_SHORT", "Nonce must be at least 8 bytes")

        val alias = "$ALIAS_PREFIX${System.currentTimeMillis()}"
        return try {
            generateAttestedKey(alias, challenge)

            val keyStore = KeyStore.getInstance(KEYSTORE).apply { load(null) }
            val chain = keyStore.getCertificateChain(alias)
                ?: throw IllegalStateException("Keystore returned no certificate chain")

            val certs = JSONArray()
            chain.forEach { certs.put(Base64.encodeToString((it as X509Certificate).encoded, Base64.NO_WRAP)) }

            okJson(
                JSONObject()
                    .put("nonce", nonce)
                    .put("chain", certs)
                    .put("chain_length", chain.size)
            )
        } catch (e: Exception) {
            Log.e(TAG, "Attestation failed", e)
            error(Response.Status.INTERNAL_ERROR, "ATTESTATION_FAILED", e.message ?: "Keystore error")
        } finally {
            runCatching {
                KeyStore.getInstance(KEYSTORE).apply { load(null) }.deleteEntry(alias)
            }
        }
    }

    private fun generateAttestedKey(alias: String, challenge: ByteArray) {
        val strongBoxAdvertised = Build.VERSION.SDK_INT >= Build.VERSION_CODES.P
        if (strongBoxAdvertised) {
            try {
                buildKey(alias, challenge, strongBox = true)
                return
            } catch (e: Exception) {
                Log.w(TAG, "StrongBox refused the key, falling back to TEE: ${e.message}")
            }
        }
        buildKey(alias, challenge, strongBox = false)
    }

    private fun buildKey(alias: String, challenge: ByteArray, strongBox: Boolean) {
        val spec = KeyGenParameterSpec.Builder(alias, KeyProperties.PURPOSE_SIGN)
            .setAlgorithmParameterSpec(ECGenParameterSpec("secp256r1"))
            .setDigests(KeyProperties.DIGEST_SHA256)
            .setAttestationChallenge(challenge)
            .setIsStrongBoxBacked(strongBox)
            .build()
        KeyPairGenerator.getInstance(KeyProperties.KEY_ALGORITHM_EC, KEYSTORE).apply {
            initialize(spec)
            generateKeyPair()
        }
    }

    /** Decodes the unpadded base64url nonce emitted by the backend /nonce endpoint. */
    private fun decodeNonce(nonce: String): ByteArray? =
        runCatching { Base64.decode(nonce, Base64.URL_SAFE or Base64.NO_WRAP) }
            .getOrNull()
            ?.takeIf { it.isNotEmpty() }

    private fun okJson(json: JSONObject): Response =
        newFixedLengthResponse(Response.Status.OK, MIME_JSON, json.toString(2))

    private fun error(status: Response.Status, code: String, message: String): Response =
        newFixedLengthResponse(
            status, MIME_JSON,
            JSONObject().put("error", code).put("message", message).toString(2)
        )
}

```

`apps/QuarkslabAttestationServer/app/src/main/java/com/quarkslab/attestation/server/MainActivity.kt`:

```kt
package com.quarkslab.attestation.server

import android.os.Bundle
import android.view.WindowManager
import androidx.appcompat.app.AppCompatActivity
import com.quarkslab.attestation.server.databinding.ActivityMainBinding
import fi.iki.elonen.NanoHTTPD.SOCKET_READ_TIMEOUT
import java.net.Inet4Address
import java.net.NetworkInterface

class MainActivity : AppCompatActivity() {

    private lateinit var binding: ActivityMainBinding
    private var server: AttestationHttpServer? = null

    override fun onCreate(savedInstanceState: Bundle?) {
        super.onCreate(savedInstanceState)
        binding = ActivityMainBinding.inflate(layoutInflater)
        setContentView(binding.root)

        // Keep the screen on while this screen is visible. This is what video
        // players do. It stops the device from entering idle and throttling the
        // socket, which is what made requests hang on the old build.
        window.addFlags(WindowManager.LayoutParams.FLAG_KEEP_SCREEN_ON)

        binding.btnToggle.setOnClickListener { if (server == null) startServer() else stopServer() }
        render()
    }

    override fun onDestroy() {
        stopServer()
        super.onDestroy()
    }

    private fun startServer() {
        val port = binding.etPort.text.toString().toIntOrNull() ?: AttestationHttpServer.DEFAULT_PORT
        try {
            server = AttestationHttpServer(port).also { it.start(SOCKET_READ_TIMEOUT, false) }
        } catch (e: Exception) {
            server = null
            binding.tvStatus.text = "FAILED: ${e.message}"
            binding.tvStatus.setTextColor(getColor(R.color.qb_orange))
            return
        }
        render()
    }

    private fun stopServer() {
        server?.stop()
        server = null
        render()
    }

    private fun render() {
        val running = server?.isAlive == true
        val port = server?.listeningPort ?: (binding.etPort.text.toString().toIntOrNull() ?: AttestationHttpServer.DEFAULT_PORT)
        val baseUrl = "http://${localIpAddress()}:$port"

        binding.tvStatus.text = if (running) "RUNNING" else "STOPPED"
        binding.tvStatus.setTextColor(getColor(if (running) R.color.qb_blue else R.color.qb_orange))

        binding.btnToggle.text = getString(if (running) R.string.stop else R.string.start)
        binding.etPort.isEnabled = !running

        binding.tvUrl.text = if (running) baseUrl else "—"
        binding.tvEndpoints.text = if (running) {
            """
            GET  $baseUrl/status
            POST $baseUrl/attest
                 { "nonce": "<base64url>" }
            GET  $baseUrl/attest?nonce=<base64url>
            """.trimIndent()
        } else {
            "Start the server to see the endpoints."
        }
    }

    /** First non loopback IPv4 address, so the URL is reachable from other hosts. */
    private fun localIpAddress(): String {
        return runCatching {
            NetworkInterface.getNetworkInterfaces().asSequence()
                .filter { it.isUp && !it.isLoopback }
                .flatMap { it.inetAddresses.asSequence() }
                .filterIsInstance<Inet4Address>()
                .firstOrNull()
                ?.hostAddress
        }.getOrNull() ?: "127.0.0.1"
    }
}

`apps/QuarkslabAttestationServer/gradlew`:

```
#!/bin/sh

#
# Copyright © 2015-2021 the original authors.
#
# Licensed under the Apache License, Version 2.0 (the "License");
# you may not use this file except in compliance with the License.
# You may obtain a copy of the License at
#
#      https://www.apache.org/licenses/LICENSE-2.0
#
# Unless required by applicable law or agreed to in writing, software
# distributed under the License is distributed on an "AS IS" BASIS,
# WITHOUT WARRANTIES OR CONDITIONS OF ANY KIND, either express or implied.
# See the License for the specific language governing permissions and
# limitations under the License.
#
# SPDX-License-Identifier: Apache-2.0
#

##############################################################################
#
#   Gradle start up script for POSIX generated by Gradle.
#
#   Important for running:
#
#   (1) You need a POSIX-compliant shell to run this script. If your /bin/sh is
#       noncompliant, but you have some other compliant shell such as ksh or
#       bash, then to run this script, type that shell name before the whole
#       command line, like:
#
#           ksh Gradle
#
#       Busybox and similar reduced shells will NOT work, because this script
#       requires all of these POSIX shell features:
#         * functions;
#         * expansions «$var», «${var}», «${var:-default}», «${var+SET}»,
#           «${var#prefix}», «${var%suffix}», and «$( cmd )»;
#         * compound commands having a testable exit status, especially «case»;
#         * various built-in commands including «command», «set», and «ulimit».
#
#   Important for patching:
#
#   (2) This script targets any POSIX shell, so it avoids extensions provided
#       by Bash, Ksh, etc; in particular arrays are avoided.
#
#       The "traditional" practice of packing multiple parameters into a
#       space-separated string is a well documented source of bugs and security
#       problems, so this is (mostly) avoided, by progressively accumulating
#       options in "$@", and eventually passing that to Java.
#
#       Where the inherited environment variables (DEFAULT_JVM_OPTS, JAVA_OPTS,
#       and GRADLE_OPTS) rely on word-splitting, this is performed explicitly;
#       see the in-line comments for details.
#
#       There are tweaks for specific operating systems such as AIX, CygWin,
#       Darwin, MinGW, and NonStop.
#
#   (3) This script is generated from the Groovy template
#       https://github.com/gradle/gradle/blob/HEAD/platforms/jvm/plugins-application/src/main/resources/org/gradle/api/internal/plugins/unixStartScript.txt
#       within the Gradle project.
#
#       You can find Gradle at https://github.com/gradle/gradle/.
#
##############################################################################

# Attempt to set APP_HOME

# Resolve links: $0 may be a link
app_path=$0

# Need this for daisy-chained symlinks.
while
    APP_HOME=${app_path%"${app_path##*/}"}  # leaves a trailing /; empty if no leading path
    [ -h "$app_path" ]
do
    ls=$( ls -ld "$app_path" )
    link=${ls#*' -> '}
    case $link in             #(
      /*)   app_path=$link ;; #(
      *)    app_path=$APP_HOME$link ;;
    esac
done

# This is normally unused
# shellcheck disable=SC2034
APP_BASE_NAME=${0##*/}
# Discard cd standard output in case $CDPATH is set (https://github.com/gradle/gradle/issues/25036)
APP_HOME=$( cd -P "${APP_HOME:-./}" > /dev/null && printf '%s\n' "$PWD" ) || exit

# Use the maximum available, or set MAX_FD != -1 to use that value.
MAX_FD=maximum

warn () {
    echo "$*"
} >&2

die () {
    echo
    echo "$*"
    echo
    exit 1
} >&2

# OS specific support (must be 'true' or 'false').
cygwin=false
msys=false
darwin=false
nonstop=false
case "$( uname )" in                #(
  CYGWIN* )         cygwin=true  ;; #(
  Darwin* )         darwin=true  ;; #(
  MSYS* | MINGW* )  msys=true    ;; #(
  NONSTOP* )        nonstop=true ;;
esac

CLASSPATH="\\\"\\\""


# Determine the Java command to use to start the JVM.
if [ -n "$JAVA_HOME" ] ; then
    if [ -x "$JAVA_HOME/jre/sh/java" ] ; then
        # IBM's JDK on AIX uses strange locations for the executables
        JAVACMD=$JAVA_HOME/jre/sh/java
    else
        JAVACMD=$JAVA_HOME/bin/java
    fi
    if [ ! -x "$JAVACMD" ] ; then
        die "ERROR: JAVA_HOME is set to an invalid directory: $JAVA_HOME

Please set the JAVA_HOME variable in your environment to match the
location of your Java installation."
    fi
else
    JAVACMD=java
    if ! command -v java >/dev/null 2>&1
    then
        die "ERROR: JAVA_HOME is not set and no 'java' command could be found in your PATH.

Please set the JAVA_HOME variable in your environment to match the
location of your Java installation."
    fi
fi

# Increase the maximum file descriptors if we can.
if ! "$cygwin" && ! "$darwin" && ! "$nonstop" ; then
    case $MAX_FD in #(
      max*)
        # In POSIX sh, ulimit -H is undefined. That's why the result is checked to see if it worked.
        # shellcheck disable=SC2039,SC3045
        MAX_FD=$( ulimit -H -n ) ||
            warn "Could not query maximum file descriptor limit"
    esac
    case $MAX_FD in  #(
      '' | soft) :;; #(
      *)
        # In POSIX sh, ulimit -n is undefined. That's why the result is checked to see if it worked.
        # shellcheck disable=SC2039,SC3045
        ulimit -n "$MAX_FD" ||
            warn "Could not set maximum file descriptor limit to $MAX_FD"
    esac
fi

# Collect all arguments for the java command, stacking in reverse order:
#   * args from the command line
#   * the main class name
#   * -classpath
#   * -D...appname settings
#   * --module-path (only if needed)
#   * DEFAULT_JVM_OPTS, JAVA_OPTS, and GRADLE_OPTS environment variables.

# For Cygwin or MSYS, switch paths to Windows format before running java
if "$cygwin" || "$msys" ; then
    APP_HOME=$( cygpath --path --mixed "$APP_HOME" )
    CLASSPATH=$( cygpath --path --mixed "$CLASSPATH" )

    JAVACMD=$( cygpath --unix "$JAVACMD" )

    # Now convert the arguments - kludge to limit ourselves to /bin/sh
    for arg do
        if
            case $arg in                                #(
              -*)   false ;;                            # don't mess with options #(
              /?*)  t=${arg#/} t=/${t%%/*}              # looks like a POSIX filepath
                    [ -e "$t" ] ;;                      #(
              *)    false ;;
            esac
        then
            arg=$( cygpath --path --ignore --mixed "$arg" )
        fi
        # Roll the args list around exactly as many times as the number of
        # args, so each arg winds up back in the position where it started, but
        # possibly modified.
        #
        # NB: a `for` loop captures its iteration list before it begins, so
        # changing the positional parameters here affects neither the number of
        # iterations, nor the values presented in `arg`.
        shift                   # remove old arg
        set -- "$@" "$arg"      # push replacement arg
    done
fi


# Add default JVM options here. You can also use JAVA_OPTS and GRADLE_OPTS to pass JVM options to this script.
DEFAULT_JVM_OPTS='"-Xmx64m" "-Xms64m"'

# Collect all arguments for the java command:
#   * DEFAULT_JVM_OPTS, JAVA_OPTS, and optsEnvironmentVar are not allowed to contain shell fragments,
#     and any embedded shellness will be escaped.
#   * For example: A user cannot expect ${Hostname} to be expanded, as it is an environment variable and will be
#     treated as '${Hostname}' itself on the command line.

set -- \
        "-Dorg.gradle.appname=$APP_BASE_NAME" \
        -classpath "$CLASSPATH" \
        -jar "$APP_HOME/gradle/wrapper/gradle-wrapper.jar" \
        "$@"

# Stop when "xargs" is not available.
if ! command -v xargs >/dev/null 2>&1
then
    die "xargs is not available"
fi

# Use "xargs" to parse quoted args.
#
# With -n1 it outputs one arg per line, with the quotes and backslashes removed.
#
# In Bash we could simply go:
#
#   readarray ARGS < <( xargs -n1 <<<"$var" ) &&
#   set -- "${ARGS[@]}" "$@"
#
# but POSIX shell has neither arrays nor command substitution, so instead we
# post-process each arg (as a line of input to sed) to backslash-escape any
# character that might be a shell metacharacter, then use eval to reverse
# that process (while maintaining the separation between arguments), and wrap
# the whole thing up as a single "set" statement.
#
# This will of course break if any of these variables contains a newline or
# an unmatched quote.
#

eval "set -- $(
        printf '%s\n' "$DEFAULT_JVM_OPTS $JAVA_OPTS $GRADLE_OPTS" |
        xargs -n1 |
        sed ' s~[^-[:alnum:]+,./:=@_]~\\&~g; ' |
        tr '\n' ' '
    )" '"$@"'

exec "$JAVACMD" "$@"

```

`backend/README.md`:

```md
# Attestation backend

> Part of the [Android hardware attestation bypass demo](../README.md). See the root README for the full setup.

Python validator for the Android Key Attestation demo. It issues a challenge,
then validates the certificate chain the Android client produces for that
challenge and reports a verdict. It is the relying party in the demo: the piece
the whole bypass has to convince.

No web framework. The server is built on the standard library `http.server`,
single threaded, state kept in memory. Chain path building and signature
verification are delegated to `certvalidator`; everything attestation specific
(extension parsing, challenge binding, root of trust, device policy) lives here.

## API

| Method | Path | Body | Response |
|--------|------|------|----------|
| POST | `/nonce` | - | `{ "nonce": "<base64url>", "expires_in": 300 }` |
| POST | `/verify` | `{ "nonce": "<base64url>", "chain": ["<der_base64>", ...] }`, leaf first | `{ "valid": true, "hardware_backed": bool, "attestation": { ... } }` or `{ "valid": false, "error": "..." }` |

The nonce is a 32-byte random value, base64url without padding. It is single
use and expires after `NONCE_TTL_SECONDS` (300). `/verify` matches the challenge
embedded in the certificate against the issued nonce, then consumes it.

## What `/verify` enforces

- **Path and signatures**: `certvalidator` builds the path to a trusted root and
  verifies every signature. At least two certificates are required.
- **Root of trust**: only the two current Google Hardware Attestation Root CAs
  are trusted (RSA and EC), embedded as PEM constants. These are public trust
  anchors, not secrets.
- **Extension selection**: the attestation extension (OID
  `1.3.6.1.4.1.11129.2.1.17`) is read from the certificate nearest the root, not
  the leaf, so a forged extension appended lower in the chain is ignored.
- **Root of trust fields**: read from the `tee_enforced` authorization list only;
  `software_enforced` is attacker controllable and ignored.
- **Challenge binding**: the challenge in the certificate must equal the issued
  nonce.
- **Revocation**: serials are checked against Google's status list
  (`https://android.googleapis.com/attestation/status`), cached per its
  `Cache-Control`. Fail closed by default (`REVOCATION_FAIL_OPEN = False`).
- **Device policy**: `REQUIRE_HARDWARE_BACKED` rejects a software security level;
  `REQUIRE_VERIFIED_BOOT` requires `device_locked == true` and a `Verified` boot
  state.

`REQUIRE_VERIFIED_BOOT` and `REQUIRE_HARDWARE_BACKED` are exactly the controls
this demo shows being bypassed: a chain from a rooted device fails them, while a
relayed chain from a clean device passes.

## Configuration

Top-of-file constants in `attestation_backend.py`:

```
HOST = "0.0.0.0"          # binds all interfaces
PORT = 8080
NONCE_TTL_SECONDS = 300
REQUIRE_VERIFIED_BOOT = True
REQUIRE_HARDWARE_BACKED = True
REVOCATION_FAIL_OPEN = False
```

State (pending nonces, cached revocation list) is in memory only and is lost on
restart. It is not shared across processes, so run a single instance.

## Install and run

Tested with **Python 3.14.6** in an isolated virtual environment (no system
site-packages).

```bash
python3 -m venv .venv
source .venv/bin/activate
pip install -r requirements.txt
python3 attestation_backend.py
```

The server prints its listen address and the enforced policy, then serves until
`Ctrl+C`. Quick check:

```bash
curl -X POST http://localhost:8080/nonce
```

Dependencies are pinned in `requirements.txt` (`asn1crypto`, `certvalidator`,
`oscrypto`; no transitive dependencies).

## Notes

- Requires a physical device on the client side. Emulators return a software
  backed chain that fails `REQUIRE_HARDWARE_BACKED`.
- Plain HTTP, no auth, no request size limits. Demo only, run it on a trusted
  local network.

```

`backend/attestation_backend.py`:

```py
#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""
Android Key Attestation demo server
===================================

Dependencies:
    pip install certvalidator asn1crypto

Routes:
    POST /nonce   -- issue a random challenge (32 bytes, base64url)
    POST /verify  -- validate the attestation chain sent by the Android client

/verify request body (JSON):
    {
        "nonce": "<base64url>",
        "chain": ["<der_base64>", "<der_base64>", ...]   # leaf first, root last
    }

Chain path building and signature verification are delegated to certvalidator
(a higher level X.509 path validator built on asn1crypto). This server adds the
attestation specific logic: revocation check against the Google status list,
extension extraction from the certificate nearest the root, challenge binding,
root of trust enforcement and security level reporting.
"""

import base64
import json
import re
import secrets
import time
import urllib.request
from http.server import BaseHTTPRequestHandler, HTTPServer

import asn1crypto.core as asn1core
from asn1crypto import x509 as asn1x509
from asn1crypto.parser import parse as asn1_parse, peek as asn1_peek
from certvalidator import CertificateValidator, ValidationContext
from certvalidator.errors import (
    InvalidCertificateError,
    PathBuildingError,
    PathValidationError,
)

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Configuration
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

HOST = "0.0.0.0"
PORT = 8080

NONCE_TTL_SECONDS = 300  # lifetime of an issued nonce

# Proprietary Google OID for the Key Attestation extension.
KEY_ATTESTATION_OID = "1.3.6.1.4.1.11129.2.1.17"

# Google certificate revocation status list.
# https://developer.android.com/privacy-and-security/security-key-attestation#certificate_status
# The endpoint returns only the certificates that are not in a normal valid
# state, keyed by lowercase hex serial number.
REVOCATION_STATUS_URL = "https://android.googleapis.com/attestation/status"
REVOCATION_FETCH_TIMEOUT = 10  # seconds
REVOCATION_DEFAULT_TTL = 3600  # fallback cache lifetime when no Cache-Control

# Fail closed: if the status list cannot be fetched, reject the attestation.
# Set to True to keep serving when the CRL endpoint is unreachable.
REVOCATION_FAIL_OPEN = False

# Device integrity policy.
#
# A valid certificate path only proves that the key lives in hardware. It says
# nothing about the state of the operating system. That information is carried
# by the RootOfTrust structure inside the tee_enforced AuthorizationList.
# A rooted device with an unlocked bootloader still produces a genuine chain,
# so without these two checks it passes validation.
REQUIRE_VERIFIED_BOOT = True  # reject unless deviceLocked and state Verified
REQUIRE_HARDWARE_BACKED = True  # reject a Software attestation security level

# Google Hardware Attestation Root CAs (trust anchors).
# https://developer.android.com/privacy-and-security/security-key-attestation#root_certificate
# certvalidator builds the path down to one of these. Add every root your test
# devices chain to. Two anchors are bundled here:
#   - the current RSA root (valid until 2042)
#   - the EC root that starts signing chains on 2026-02-01
# Legacy roots (2016/2019/2021) are omitted. The 2016 root expired in May 2026
# and would trip certvalidator's validity check. Devices launched before 2021
# still chain to it. To support them, add that PEM and pass whitelisted_certs
# or a fixed moment so the expiry is ignored (see the Android docs note on
# SERIALNUMBER=f92009e853b6b045).

GOOGLE_ROOT_CA_RSA_PEM = b"""\
-----BEGIN CERTIFICATE-----

"""

GOOGLE_ROOT_CA_EC_PEM = b"""\

Eczm34A5FNijV3s0/f0UPl7zbJcTx6xwqMIRq6NCMEAwDwYDVR0TAQH/BAUwAwEB
/zAOBgNVHQ8BAf8EBAMCAQYwHQYDVR0OBBYEFFIyuyz7RkOb3NaBqQ5lZuA0QepA
MAoGCCqGSM49BAMDA2gAMGUCMETfjPO/HwqReR2CS7p0ZWoD/LHs6hDi422opifH
EUaYLxwGlT9SLdjkVpz0UUOR5wIxAIoGyxGKRHVTpqpGRFiJtQEOOTp/+s1GcxeY
uR2zh/80lQyu9vAFCj6E4AXc+osmRg==
-----END CERTIFICATE-----
"""

TRUST_ROOTS = [GOOGLE_ROOT_CA_RSA_PEM, GOOGLE_ROOT_CA_EC_PEM]

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# In memory nonce store
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

# { nonce_b64url: expiry_monotonic }
_pending_nonces: dict[str, float] = {}


def _purge_expired_nonces() -> None:
    """Drop every stored nonce whose TTL has elapsed."""
    now = time.monotonic()
    for key in [k for k, exp in _pending_nonces.items() if exp < now]:
        del _pending_nonces[key]


def issue_nonce() -> str:
    """Generate a 32 byte nonce, store it and return its base64url form."""
    _purge_expired_nonces()
    raw = secrets.token_bytes(32)
    nonce = base64.urlsafe_b64encode(raw).rstrip(b"=").decode()
    _pending_nonces[nonce] = time.monotonic() + NONCE_TTL_SECONDS
    return nonce


def consume_nonce(nonce: str) -> bool:
    """Consume a nonce (single use). Return False if unknown or expired."""
    _purge_expired_nonces()
    expiry = _pending_nonces.pop(nonce, None)
    return expiry is not None and time.monotonic() <= expiry


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Key Attestation extension (ASN.1)
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

# AuthorizationList tag holding the RootOfTrust structure.
ROOT_OF_TRUST_TAG = 704


class SecurityLevel(asn1core.Enumerated):
    """Keymaster/KeyMint security level enumeration."""

    _map = {0: "Software", 1: "TrustedEnvironment", 2: "StrongBox"}


class VerifiedBootState(asn1core.Enumerated):
    """Verified Boot state enumeration."""

    _map = {0: "Verified", 1: "SelfSigned", 2: "Unverified", 3: "Failed"}


class RootOfTrust(asn1core.Sequence):
    """RootOfTrust structure from the Keymaster/KeyMint schemas."""

    # verified_boot_hash only exists from KeyMint (attestation version 4)
    # onward, so it stays optional.
    _fields = [
        ("verified_boot_key", asn1core.OctetString),
        ("device_locked", asn1core.Boolean),
        ("verified_boot_state", VerifiedBootState),
        ("verified_boot_hash", asn1core.OctetString, {"optional": True}),
    ]


class KeyDescription(asn1core.Sequence):
    """Minimal KeyDescription: only the fields we need are typed."""

    # The two AuthorizationList sequences are read as Any so we do not have to
    # model the full schema; the remaining fields bind the challenge and report
    # the security level.
    _fields = [
        ("attestation_version", asn1core.Integer),
        ("attestation_security_level", SecurityLevel),
        ("keymaster_version", asn1core.Integer),
        ("keymaster_security_level", SecurityLevel),
        ("attestation_challenge", asn1core.OctetString),
        ("unique_id", asn1core.OctetString),
        ("software_enforced", asn1core.Any),
        ("tee_enforced", asn1core.Any),
    ]


def _iter_authorizations(seq_der: bytes):
    """Yield (tag_number, inner_der) for every AuthorizationList entry."""
    # Every field of an AuthorizationList is a context specific EXPLICIT tag,
    # so an entry's contents is the complete DER encoding of the inner value.
    _, _, _, _, contents, _ = asn1_parse(seq_der)
    while contents:
        size = asn1_peek(contents)
        _, _, tag, _, value, _ = asn1_parse(contents[:size])
        yield tag, value
        contents = contents[size:]


def _extract_root_of_trust(tee_enforced_der: bytes) -> dict | None:
    """Return the RootOfTrust from the tee_enforced list, or None if absent."""
    # Reading it from tee_enforced is the whole point: the software_enforced
    # list is populated by the Android framework and an attacker controlling
    # the OS can write anything into it. Only the tee_enforced copy is produced
    # by the secure environment.
    for tag, value in _iter_authorizations(tee_enforced_der):
        if tag != ROOT_OF_TRUST_TAG:
            continue
        try:
            rot = RootOfTrust.load(value)
            rot.native  # force the decoding so a bad shape raises here
        except Exception:
            # A few implementations wrap the structure in one extra SEQUENCE.
            _, _, _, _, inner, _ = asn1_parse(value)
            rot = RootOfTrust.load(inner)
        return {
            "verified_boot_key": rot["verified_boot_key"].native.hex(),
            "device_locked": bool(rot["device_locked"].native),
            "verified_boot_state": rot["verified_boot_state"].native,
        }
    return None


def _parse_key_description(extn_value: bytes) -> dict:
    """Parse a KeyDescription extension value into a plain dict."""
    kd = KeyDescription.load(extn_value)
    challenge = bytes(kd["attestation_challenge"].native)
    parsed = {
        "attestation_version": int(kd["attestation_version"].native),
        "attestation_security_level": kd["attestation_security_level"].native,
        "keymaster_version": int(kd["keymaster_version"].native),
        "keymaster_security_level": kd["keymaster_security_level"].native,
        "challenge_b64": base64.urlsafe_b64encode(challenge).rstrip(b"=").decode(),
    }
    root_of_trust = _extract_root_of_trust(kd["tee_enforced"].dump())
    if root_of_trust is None:
        parsed["verified_boot_state"] = "absent"
        parsed["device_locked"] = None
        parsed["verified_boot_key"] = None
    else:
        parsed["verified_boot_state"] = root_of_trust["verified_boot_state"]
        parsed["device_locked"] = root_of_trust["device_locked"]
        parsed["verified_boot_key"] = root_of_trust["verified_boot_key"]
    return parsed


def extract_key_description(chain: list[asn1x509.Certificate]) -> dict | None:
    """Parse the KeyDescription from the extension nearest the root."""
    # Only the occurrence nearest the root is trustworthy: an attacker can
    # append certificates toward the leaf carrying a forged extension, so we
    # never trust an occurrence closer to the leaf. None if none carries it.
    for cert in reversed(chain):  # root first, then toward the leaf
        for ext in cert["tbs_certificate"]["extensions"]:
            if ext["extn_id"].dotted == KEY_ATTESTATION_OID:
                return _parse_key_description(ext["extn_value"].native)
    return None


def check_device_integrity(attestation: dict) -> str | None:
    """Apply the device policy; return an error string, or None if acceptable."""
    if REQUIRE_HARDWARE_BACKED:
        level = attestation["attestation_security_level"]
        if level not in ("TrustedEnvironment", "StrongBox"):
            return f"Attestation security level is {level}, hardware backing required"

    if not REQUIRE_VERIFIED_BOOT:
        return None

    state = attestation["verified_boot_state"]
    if state == "absent":
        return "RootOfTrust missing from the tee_enforced authorization list"
    if attestation["device_locked"] is not True:
        return "Bootloader is unlocked (deviceLocked is false)"
    if state != "Verified":
        return f"Verified Boot state is {state}, expected Verified"
    return None


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Revocation (Google attestation status list)
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

# { 'fetched_at': monotonic, 'ttl': seconds, 'entries': dict | None }
_revocation_cache: dict = {"fetched_at": 0.0, "ttl": 0.0, "entries": None}


def fetch_revocation_entries() -> dict:
    """Return the status list entries, honouring the response Cache-Control."""
    # Raises on network or decode error so the caller can fail closed.
    now = time.monotonic()
    cache = _revocation_cache
    if cache["entries"] is not None and (now - cache["fetched_at"]) < cache["ttl"]:
        return cache["entries"]

    req = urllib.request.Request(
        REVOCATION_STATUS_URL, headers={"User-Agent": "attestation-demo"}
    )
    with urllib.request.urlopen(req, timeout=REVOCATION_FETCH_TIMEOUT) as resp:
        raw = resp.read()
        match = re.search(r"max-age=(\d+)", resp.headers.get("Cache-Control", ""))
        ttl = float(match.group(1)) if match else float(REVOCATION_DEFAULT_TTL)

    entries = json.loads(raw).get("entries", {})
    cache.update(fetched_at=now, ttl=ttl, entries=entries)
    return entries


def check_revocation(chain: list[asn1x509.Certificate]) -> str | None:
    """Return an error string if any certificate serial is revoked, else None."""
    # Also returns an error when the list cannot be fetched and
    # REVOCATION_FAIL_OPEN is False (fail closed).
    try:
        entries = fetch_revocation_entries()
    except Exception as exc:
        if REVOCATION_FAIL_OPEN:
            return None
        return f"Cannot fetch revocation status list: {exc}"

    for cert in chain:
        serial_hex = "%x" % cert.serial_number  # lowercase hex, no leading zero
        entry = entries.get(serial_hex)
        if entry is not None:
            return (
                f'Certificate {serial_hex} is {entry.get("status", "listed")} '
                f'(reason: {entry.get("reason", "n/a")})'
            )
    return None


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Chain validation
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------


def validate_attestation_chain(chain_der: list[bytes]) -> dict:
    """Validate the path with certvalidator and parse the attestation extension."""
    # Returns { ok, error, attestation }.
    if len(chain_der) < 2:
        return {
            "ok": False,
            "error": "Chain must contain at least 2 certificates",
            "attestation": None,
        }

    end_entity, *intermediates = chain_der

    context = ValidationContext(
        trust_roots=TRUST_ROOTS,
        allow_fetching=False,
        revocation_mode="soft-fail",
    )
    validator = CertificateValidator(
        end_entity, intermediates, validation_context=context
    )

    try:
        # Empty key usage set: attestation leaves carry no serverAuth EKU.
        validator.validate_usage(set())
    except (PathBuildingError, PathValidationError, InvalidCertificateError) as exc:
        return {
            "ok": False,
            "error": f"Chain validation failed: {exc}",
            "attestation": None,
        }

    chain = [asn1x509.Certificate.load(der) for der in chain_der]

    # Reject if any certificate in the chain is revoked or suspended.
    revoked = check_revocation(chain)
    if revoked is not None:
        return {"ok": False, "error": revoked, "attestation": None}

    try:
        attestation = extract_key_description(chain)
    except Exception as exc:
        return {
            "ok": False,
            "error": f"KeyDescription parsing failed: {exc}",
            "attestation": None,
        }

    if attestation is None:
        return {
            "ok": False,
            "error": "Key Attestation extension missing from the chain",
            "attestation": None,
        }

    return {"ok": True, "error": None, "attestation": attestation}


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# HTTP handler
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------


class AttestationHandler(BaseHTTPRequestHandler):
    """HTTP handler exposing POST /nonce and POST /verify."""

    def log_message(self, fmt, *args):
        """Log a request line to stdout, prefixed with the client address."""
        print(f"  [{self.address_string()}] {fmt % args}")

    def _read_json_body(self) -> dict | None:
        """Read and decode the JSON request body; None on invalid JSON."""
        length = int(self.headers.get("Content-Length", 0))
        if length == 0:
            return {}
        try:
            return json.loads(self.rfile.read(length))
        except json.JSONDecodeError:
            return None

    def _send_json(self, status: int, payload: dict) -> None:
        """Serialise payload as JSON and write the HTTP response."""
        body = json.dumps(payload, indent=2).encode()
        self.send_response(status)
        self.send_header("Content-Type", "application/json")
        self.send_header("Content-Length", str(len(body)))
        self.end_headers()
        self.wfile.write(body)

    def do_POST(self):
        """Route POST /nonce and POST /verify."""
        if self.path == "/nonce":
            self._handle_nonce()
        elif self.path == "/verify":
            self._handle_verify()
        else:
            self._send_json(404, {"error": "Unknown route"})

    def _handle_nonce(self):
        """Issue a fresh nonce."""
        self._send_json(200, {"nonce": issue_nonce(), "expires_in": NONCE_TTL_SECONDS})

    def _handle_verify(self):
        """Validate a submitted chain against the nonce and device policy."""
        body = self._read_json_body()
        if body is None:
            self._send_json(400, {"error": "Invalid JSON body"})
            return

        nonce = body.get("nonce", "")
        chain_b64 = body.get("chain", [])

        if not nonce:
            self._send_json(400, {"error": 'Missing "nonce" field'})
            return
        if not isinstance(chain_b64, list) or not chain_b64:
            self._send_json(400, {"error": 'Missing or empty "chain" field'})
            return

        try:
            chain_der = [base64.b64decode(c) for c in chain_b64]
        except Exception:
            self._send_json(400, {"error": 'Invalid base64 in "chain"'})
            return

        result = validate_attestation_chain(chain_der)
        if not result["ok"]:
            self._send_json(400, {"valid": False, "error": result["error"]})
            return

        attestation = result["attestation"]

        # Bind the attestation to the challenge we issued.
        if attestation["challenge_b64"] != nonce:
            self._send_json(
                400,
                {
                    "valid": False,
                    "error": "Attestation challenge does not match issued nonce",
                    "attestation": attestation,
                },
            )
            return

        # Single use, also enforces expiry.
        if not consume_nonce(nonce):
            self._send_json(
                400,
                {
                    "valid": False,
                    "error": "Unknown or expired nonce",
                    "attestation": attestation,
                },
            )
            return

        # Device policy: hardware backing and Verified Boot state.
        integrity_error = check_device_integrity(attestation)
        if integrity_error is not None:
            self._send_json(
                400,
                {
                    "valid": False,
                    "error": integrity_error,
                    "attestation": attestation,
                },
            )
            return

        sec_level = attestation["attestation_security_level"]
        self._send_json(
            200,
            {
                "valid": True,
                "hardware_backed": sec_level in ("TrustedEnvironment", "StrongBox"),
                "attestation": attestation,
            },
        )


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Entry point
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

if __name__ == "__main__":
    server = HTTPServer((HOST, PORT), AttestationHandler)
    print(f"Server listening on http://{HOST}:{PORT}")
    print("  POST /nonce  -- issue a challenge")
    print("  POST /verify -- validate an attestation")
    print(f"  verified boot enforced: {REQUIRE_VERIFIED_BOOT}")
    print("Ctrl+C to stop\n")
    try:
        server.serve_forever()
    except KeyboardInterrupt:
        print("\nShutting down.")

```

`backend/requirements.txt`:

```txt
asn1crypto==1.5.1

`instrumentation/README.md`:

```md
# Attestation relay instrumentation

> Part of the [Android hardware attestation bypass demo](../README.md). See the root README for the full setup.

Frida instrumentation for the Android hardware attestation demo. It redirects
the demo client local Keystore attestation to a clean device, so the chain sent
to the backend reflects a locked bootloader.

This is the analyst side of the demo. The scenario is an analyst on a rooted
mission phone whose target app uses hardware attestation. The relay lets the
analysis continue.

## Pieces

```
agent/agent.ts   Frida agent, hooks the client attestation seam
control.py       Controller, relays the nonce to the clean device
```

The clean device runs the Quarkslab Attestation Server app. The backend is the
Python validator with POST /nonce and POST /verify.

## Hook point

The agent hooks `KeystoreAttestation.generateAttestedKey(challenge)` in
`com.quarkslab.attestation.demo`. It does not call the original, so the local
Keystore is never used. It re-encodes the challenge bytes to unpadded base64url,
the exact nonce string the backend issued, queries the clean device, and returns
the relayed chain as an `AttestedKey`.

## Build

Build on Node 20 or 22 LTS. Frida 17.16.4 ships prebuilt native bindings for
LTS Node only. On a bleeding edge Node such as 26 there is no prebuild, so
`frida-compile` fails to load `frida_binding.node` and the build dies with a
bindings error. An `.nvmrc` pins the version.

```shell
nvm use            # switches to Node 22 from .nvmrc
npm install
npm run build      # produces _agent.js next to control.py
```

Stack: frida 17.16.4, frida-compile 19, frida-java-bridge 7.

## Run

1. Start the backend validator and the clean device server app.
2. Note the clean device IP shown in the server app, for example 172.22.0.15.
3. Set up the controller in an isolated virtual environment (once):

```shell
python3 -m venv .venv
source .venv/bin/activate
pip install -r requirements.txt
```

4. Spawn the demo client under the agent and relay:

```shell
python3 control.py --host 172.22.0.15 --port 8080
```

The controller spawns the app, installs the hook, then resumes it. Leave it
running.

5. Tap RUN ATTESTATION in the demo client. The backend returns valid.

## Requirements

- Frida server 17.16.4 running on the rooted analysis device.
- Python 3.14.6 for the controller (the version used in testing), with the
  pinned packages in `requirements.txt` (`frida`, `requests`) installed in an
  isolated virtual environment.
- Node 20 or 22 LTS for the agent build. See the Build section.

```
